# MedVision re-validation — docsite + skills vs code, medvision_ds v1.4.0, and the paper

Method: 18 parallel Opus auditors, each finding then re-checked by an independent verifier told to refute it.
Sources of truth: `Github/MedVision` (medvision_bm, working tree), `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision` (medvision_ds v1.4.0), `MedVision-EMNLP26-v1/latex/paper.tex`, and `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/verl`.

**Result: 129 raw -> 128 surviving (7 high, 57 medium, 64 low); 15 regressions; 1 refuted.** 18/18 units returned.

By source: paper alignment 22, medvision_ds 18, medvision_bm regression 88.


---

## HIGH (7)

### H1. `docsite/source/reference/cli.md:184`
*docsite:reference (bm) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** `eval__kimi` is listed inside the "vLLM / local-weight drivers" code block (line 184), under the preceding sentence "The following modules follow the same command shape — the vLLM shape for local-weight models, the API shape for hosted ones". The "API drivers" block (cli.md:196-198) lists only `eval__openai`, `eval__claude`, `eval__gemini`.
- **Truth:** src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__kimi.py:17-20 defines `API_KEY_ENV_VARS = {"moonshot": "MOONSHOT_API_KEY", "openrouter": "OPENROUTER_API_KEY"}`; :66-82 add `--kimi_model_code` and `--api_provider` (choices moonshot/openrouter); :207-210 hard-fail when the provider key env var is unset; :23 defines `run_evaluation_for_task_API_models`. Its full flag set is the API shape (`--kimi_model_code --api_provider --model_name --max_tokens --reshape_image_hw --batch_size ...`) with NO `--lmmseval_module`, `--model_hf_id`, `--dtype`, `--lora_path`, `--batch_size_per_gpu` or `--gpu_memory_utilization`. The model wrapper is src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/models/kimi.py (sibling of claude.py/gemini.py/openai.py, not a vllm_*.py). This also contradicts two sibling files in the same unit: releases/index.md:26 ("API-model evaluators (Claude, Gemini, GPT, Kimi)") and releases/v1.1.1.md:12 ("Kimi K2.6: OpenAI-compatible API model").
- **Fix:** Move `eval__kimi` out of the vLLM/local-weight code block at cli.md:182-188 and into the "API drivers" block at cli.md:197-199 (`eval__openai eval__claude eval__gemini eval__kimi`). Optionally add a tip like the `eval__gemini` one noting Kimi's provider flags (`--kimi_model_code`, `--api_provider` moonshot|openrouter, `MOONSHOT_API_KEY`).

### H2. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:12`
*ds:filters-annotation (ds) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "**Dataset (v1.0.0):** 22 public datasets, **30.8M image-annotation pairs**"
- **Truth:** The paper states no such number. /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision-EMNLP26-v1/latex/paper.tex:91 and :101 both say "22 public datasets … **29.0K** 3D images, **11.2M** annotated 2D slices, and **24.3M** single-instance annotations", and paper.tex:572 gives "24.3M single-instance / 45.3M multi-instance". `grep -rn '30\.8'` over the whole latex/ directory returns nothing. The v1.0.0 totals in docsite/source/dataset/statistics.md (24,276,501 single / 45,314,742 multi) agree with the paper, not with 30.8M. This skill's own description says to use it "when writing README/webpage/docs text … when scale numbers matter", so the wrong number propagates directly into published text.
- **Fix:** Replace with the paper's v1.0.0 figures: "22 public datasets, 29.0K 3D images, 11.2M annotated 2D slices, 24.3M single-instance (45.3M multi-instance) annotations".

### H3. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:12`
*paper:dataset (paper) · wrong-number · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "**Dataset (v1.0.0):** 22 public datasets, **30.8M image-annotation pairs**" — the skill's only dataset-scale number, presented as the v1.0.0 published scale.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:91 (abstract) and :101 (intro): "22 public datasets ... with **29.0K** 3D images, **11.2M** annotated 2D slices, and **24.3M** single-instance annotations" (footnote: "Dataset statistics for v1.0.0"). paper.tex:632, Table appendix:tab:dataset "Total (22)" row: 29,031 3D images; single-instance 24,236,327 Box + 32,249 T/L + 7,925 A/D = 24,276,501 (24.3M); multi-instance 45,274,250 + 32,567 + 7,925 = 45,314,742 (45.3M). paper.tex:572 restates "24.3M single-instance / 45.3M multi-instance". No figure anywhere near 30.8M appears in the paper; the only occurrence of "30.8M" in the repo is the ignored dev note docs/dev/literature-review__quantitative-medical-VLM.md:41. The already-fixed docsite carries the right numbers (docs/blog/MedVision-Dataset-Tutorial.md:14-17: 29,031 / 11.2M / 24.3M / 45.3M), so the paper skill is now the one out-of-date authority.
- **Fix:** Replace "30.8M image-annotation pairs" with the paper's triple: "29.0K 3D images, 11.2M annotated 2D slices, 24.3M single-instance annotations (45.3M multi-instance)".

### H4. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:27`
*paper:methods (paper) · contradicts-paper · adjusted*

- **Claim:** RFT: "stages 1-2 use the 5.5K single-task sets, stage 3 the 1M detection set, and the released model is stage 3's `global_step_250`."
- **Truth:** paper.tex:210 (\section{MedVision-V0 Training}, RFT via GRPO): "The same 121K training samples from the SFT stage are used; the image size and prompt remain the same, while the CoT answer is removed in RFT data as models learn from the reward signal." The paper nowhere mentions a 1M detection set (grep for "1M"/"1,000,000" in paper.tex returns nothing) and nowhere names a released checkpoint step. The 1M figure and step 250 come only from the as-run recipe: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/verl/examples/grpo_trainer/train__rft-sequential__3-detection.sh:6-8 ("the paper's model is global_step_250 of this stage"; "dataset variant .. ds__AD0_D1000000_TL0_all1000000__resized-hw-512x512 (1M detection set, shards/)"), vs. train__rft-sequential__1-AD.sh:8 (5500) and 2-TL.sh:8 (5500).
- **Fix:** Make the divergence explicit instead of presenting 1M as a paper fact, e.g.: "stages 1-2 use the 5.5K single-task sets; the paper states RFT reuses the same 121K SFT samples, but the as-run stage-3 recipe (train__rft-sequential__3-detection.sh) trains on a 1M-sample detection set and the released model is that stage's global_step_250 (code, not paper)."
- **Verifier adjustment:** skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:27 presents code-only facts as paper facts (severity: medium). Reword to mark the divergence, e.g. "the paper states RFT reuses the same 121K SFT samples (paper.tex:210); the as-run recipes instead use single-task sets — stages 1-2 the 5.5K A/D and T/L parquets, stage 3 the 1M-cap detection parquet ds__AD0_D1000000_TL0_all1000000 — and the released model is that stage's global_step_250 (train__rft-sequential__{1,2,3}*.sh headers; code, not paper)."

### H5. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:12`
*paper:methods (paper) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Dataset (v1.0.0): 22 public datasets, **30.8M image-annotation pairs**"
- **Truth:** paper.tex:101 (Introduction) and the abstract (paper.tex:~94): "22 public datasets ... comprising 29.0K 3D images, 11.2M annotated 2D slices, and 24.3M single-instance annotations", footnoted "Dataset statistics for v1.0.0". The full v1.0.0 table total (paper.tex:632) is 29,031 volumes / 24,236,327 Box + 32,249 T/L + 7,925 A/D single-instance (= 24.28M) and 45,274,250 + 32,567 + 7,925 multi-instance (= 45.31M). No total in the paper equals 30.8M. The repo's own docsite already uses the paper numbers (docs/blog/MedVision-Dataset-Tutorial.md:14-16: 29,031 / 11.2M / 24.3M).
- **Fix:** Replace "30.8M image-annotation pairs" with the paper's v1.0.0 headline: 29.0K 3D images, 11.2M annotated 2D slices, 24.3M single-instance annotations (45.3M multi-instance). The same stale 30.8M also survives in docs/model-release/MedVision-V0-7B_ModelCard.md:70.

### H6. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:metrics-results (paper) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "SR (success rate) = fraction of outputs with the required numeric values parseable from `<answer></answer>`." — SR is defined as tag-bound, in the same sentence that ends "Reported numbers are computed from the LLM-judge re-parsed records ... not from the strict-regex pass alone."
- **Truth:** paper.tex:786 defines SR with no tag restriction: "success rate is defined as the proportion of model responses where the required numeric values can be parsed". paper.tex:709 makes the tag-bound reading explicitly WRONG for every reported SR: "The rule-based parser accepts only answers written inside the <answer></answer> tags, so a response that states a correct measurement in any other form (e.g., \boxed{...}, 'Answer: ...', or plain text) is scored as a parsing failure ... We therefore re-read every response with an LLM parser (Gemma4, 31B) ... and compute all reported metrics from the re-parsed records." The pooled SR in Table tab:llm_parsing_sr rises 92.2->98.6 (detection), 81.7->94.5 (T/L), 79.0->93.9 (A/D) precisely because answers stated OUTSIDE the tags now count as successes (e.g. Qwen2.5-VL 32B T/L 16.4 -> 99.9). Code confirms the reported path: script/llm-parsing/apply_judge.py:354 replaces `filtered_resps` with `LLM_filtered_resps` before summarization (judge_config.py:353-354).
- **Fix:** Reword to: "SR = fraction of responses from which the required numeric values could be parsed — by the strict parser only from `<answer></answer>`, and in the reported LLM-parsed metrics from anywhere in the response, provided the LLM-located quote is verified and the numbers re-extracted from the response text." Optionally add the pooled uplift (92.2->98.6 / 81.7->94.5 / 79.0->93.9).

### H7. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/installation.md:138`
*skills:data-env (bm) · broken-command · confirmed*

- **Claim:** §4d documents `python -m medvision_bm.sft.env_setup --data_dir <data_dir> -r <repo>/requirements/requirements_sft_<model>.txt --lmms_eval_opt_deps qwen2_5_vl` (and §4b line 116 the eval equivalent `pip install -r <repo>/requirements/requirements_eval_<model>.txt --no-deps`) as the recommended, reproducible way to apply a frozen pin set; requirements-catalog.md:3 describes all 25 files as frozen per-model pin sets with no caveat.
- **Truth:** Three of the 25 requirements files contain machine-local paths that cannot resolve anywhere else. requirements/requirements_sft_gemma4.txt:83 `-e /home/vincent/Documents/Tools/miniconda3/envs/sft-gemma4/lib/python3.11/site-packages/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval`, :92 `medvision_bm @ file:///tmp/medvision_build.j942DS/wh/medvision_bm-1.1.1-py3-none-any.whl`, :93 `medvision_ds @ file:///mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/Data/src/wheels/medvision_ds-1.1.1-py3-none-any.whl`; requirements/requirements_sft_medgemma.txt:83/92/93 the same shape (`file:///tmp/medvision_build.T7N3e5/...`); requirements/requirements_eval_llavamed.txt:97 `llava_med @ file:///root/Documents/MedVision/third_party/LLaVA-Med`. The `/tmp/medvision_build.<random>` directories are mktemp dirs the launcher deletes on exit (script/benchmark-TL/eval__Qwen-2.5-VL__TL.sh:38-39 `build_tmp=$(mktemp -d ...)` + `trap 'rm -rf "${build_tmp}"' EXIT`), so they can never exist at install time. `run_pip_install` passes the file straight to pip (src/medvision_bm/utils/install_utils.py:29-45 `pip install --upgrade --force-reinstall --no-deps -r <file>`), so the documented command aborts on the missing path before any pin is applied.
- **Fix:** Add a caveat where the `-r` / `pip install -r` commands are given (installation.md:116 and :138) and to requirements-catalog.md:3: `requirements_sft_gemma4.txt`, `requirements_sft_medgemma.txt` and `requirements_eval_llavamed.txt` are `pip freeze` dumps that still carry machine-local `-e /home/...` and `file:///...` entries (medvision_bm/medvision_ds wheels, the vendored engine, LLaVA-Med); strip or comment those lines before installing, or install the file with `grep -v -e '^-e ' -e 'file:///'`.

## MEDIUM (57)

### M1. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:22`
*docsite:bench (bm) · wrong-path · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`MedVision_ACK_RELEASE` — set only in `script/benchmark-TL/`" (line 22), reinforced by the code-fence comment `# script/benchmark-TL/ only` (line 19) and by "release 1.4.0 regenerated only the tumour/lesion annotations, so only they need it" (line 15).
- **Truth:** `grep -rl MedVision_ACK_RELEASE script/benchmark-* script/ablation/OOD` returns 28 files: the 24 T/L launchers PLUS all 4 OOD launchers. script/ablation/OOD/eval__MedVision-V0-7B__detect__planeOOD.sh:51 and .../detect__taskOOD.sh:51 both `export MedVision_ACK_RELEASE='1.1.1'` — and those are Detection, not T/L. .../TL__planeOOD.sh:53 and .../TL__taskOOD.sh:53 also set '1.1.1', not '1.4.0'. This contradicts line 5 of the same page, which explicitly introduces script/ablation/OOD as "a fourth set" of launchers. It also contradicts "its value is always the *current latest* (`1.4.0`), whatever version you pin": /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:836 accepts EITHER the release version OR the per-(dataset, plan-kind) newest annotation version, which is why the OOD launchers legitimately use 1.1.1.
- **Fix:** Reword to "set in the 24 `script/benchmark-TL/` launchers and in all 4 `script/ablation/OOD/` launchers", and replace "its value is always the current latest (1.4.0)" with the actual rule: the acknowledgement accepts either the whole-release version or the newest annotation published for the dataset being loaded (the OOD launchers use `1.1.1`).

### M2. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:18`
*docsite:bench (bm) · wrong-number · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION` ... is exported by all 72 launchers" (line 22) and the code-fence comment `export MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION='1.0.0' # every launcher` (line 18).
- **Truth:** 76 launcher scripts export the variable, not 72: `for d in benchmark-AD benchmark-TL benchmark-detect ablation/OOD; do grep -rl MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION $d | wc -l; done` → 24 / 24 / 24 / 4. And the value is not '1.0.0' in every launcher: script/ablation/OOD/eval__MedVision-V0-7B__TL__planeOOD.sh:52 and .../TL__taskOOD.sh:52 both `export MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION='1.1.1'` (74 use '1.0.0', 2 use '1.1.1'). Line 5 of this same page counts the OOD set as launchers.
- **Fix:** Say "exported by all 76 launchers" and change the fence comment to `# every launcher (the 2 T/L OOD launchers pin '1.1.1')`, or scope both statements explicitly to "the 72 benchmark-* launchers".

### M3. `docsite/source/benchmarking/parsing-and-summarizing.md:42`
*docsite:bench (bm) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The parse/summarize examples target `Results/MedVision-detect-v2` (line 42), `Results/MedVision-AD-v2-CoT` (line 79) and `Results/MedVision-TL-v2-CoT` (line 83) — but running-evaluations.md tells the user to evaluate into `Results/MedVision-AD-CoT` (lines 49, 111).
- **Truth:** All 72 benchmark launchers set the non-`v2` tags: `grep -rh '^task_tag=' script/benchmark-{AD,TL,detect}` → 24× "MedVision-AD-CoT", 24× "MedVision-TL-CoT", 24× "MedVision-detect-CoT" (e.g. script/benchmark-AD/eval__MedVision-V0-7B__AD.sh:23). The `-v2` names are what the downstream tooling defaults to (script/llm-parsing/judge_config.py:534-536, script/analyze/clinical-decision-analysis/run_CDA_analysis.sh:16-17). A user who follows running-evaluations.md and then copies the parse command hits `os.scandir` on a missing directory (src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:54) and gets FileNotFoundError.
- **Fix:** Use one tag convention across the two pages, or add a one-line note in parsing-and-summarizing.md that `-v2*` are the authors' canonical result trees while the shipped launchers write `MedVision-{AD,TL,detect}-CoT`, so `--task_dir` must match whatever `task_tag`/`--results_dir` the run used.

### M4. `docsite/source/benchmarking/llm-judge-parsing.md:89`
*docsite:bench (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "the driver forces `TP=1` unless you set `TP` or `GPU_NUM`" — implying that setting `GPU_NUM` raises the tensor-parallel degree.
- **Truth:** script/llm-parsing/run_llm_parsing.sh:109 sets `TP="${TP:-1}"` before the GPU_NUM block, and lines 115-123 use GPU_NUM only to derive `NUM_SHARDS=$(( GPU_NUM / TP ))`. TP is never derived from GPU_NUM, so `GPU_NUM=4` still runs TP=1 with 4 shards — the whole reader still lands on one card. This also contradicts two later statements in the same file: line 128 ("`TP` … Default **1** … raise it only to fit the weights") and line 134 ("The defaults are single-GPU (`TP=1`, `NUM_SHARDS=1`). Set `GPU_NUM` to spread the work: … each process spans `TP` GPUs"). Introduced by this fix pass (the pre-edit text said "default per reader; 2 for gemma-4-31b").
- **Fix:** Change to "the driver forces `TP=1` unless you set `TP`" — `GPU_NUM` only adds shards, it never widens a shard.

### M5. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:339`
*docsite:bench (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** MiniMax-M3 dropdown (added in this fix pass): "the launchers create a **Python 3.12** env and raise the budget to 16000 tokens", with the example command using `--max_new_tokens 16000` (line 345).
- **Truth:** All six MiniMax launchers set 16384, not 16000: script/benchmark-AD/eval__MiniMax-M3__AD.sh:98, eval__MiniMax-M3-INT4__AD.sh:96, script/benchmark-TL/eval__MiniMax-M3__TL.sh:98, eval__MiniMax-M3-INT4__TL.sh:96, script/benchmark-detect/eval__MiniMax-M3__detect.sh:98, eval__MiniMax-M3-INT4__detect.sh:96 — all `max_new_tokens=16384`, passed at line 151/149 as `--max_new_tokens "$max_new_tokens"`. (The Python 3.12 half is correct: eval__MiniMax-M3__AD.sh:10 `conda create -n "${ENV_NAME}" python==3.12 -y`.)
- **Fix:** Change both "16000 tokens" and `--max_new_tokens 16000` to `16384`.

### M6. `docsite/source/dataset/statistics.md:118`
*docsite:dataset (bm) · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "the donut figures show the same split — the outer ring by dataset, the inner ring by task." (i.e. the donut's two rings are claimed to be outer=dataset / inner=task, showing the same Box / T/L / A/D split as the table cells)
- **Truth:** The rings are the other way round, and the outer ring is not a task split at all. /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/src/medvision_bm/utils/summarize_datasets.py:1034-1042 (`viz_rings` docstring): "* inner — one wedge per dataset, angle proportional to ``n_benchmark_annotations`` ... * outer — that dataset's BoxSize annotations split by sub-label (anatomy)". Same at summarize_datasets.py:966-969 (`_draw_donut`: "Inner ring: one wedge per dataset ... Outer ring: that dataset's sub-labels (``_bench_anatomy``)") and summarize_datasets.py:790-800 (`_bench_anatomy` returns `boxsize_by_anatomy`). script/misc/summarize_datasets.sh (Output figures section, item 3-A) repeats it verbatim. The embedded SVG is that exact figure: `diff` shows docsite/source/_static/datasets_summary/v1.4.0_filtered.svg is byte-identical to dataset-info/datasets_summary_v1.4.0/dataset_summary_rings_filtered_2x1.svg.
- **Fix:** Replace with: "the donut figures split the same totals a different way — the inner ring one wedge per dataset (angle ∝ that dataset's benchmark-annotation count), the outer ring that dataset's BoxSize annotations broken down by anatomy."

### M7. `docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md:79`
*docsite:dataset (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The T/L generation-time floor is stated unconditionally: "A component is measured only when its fitted ellipse's **major axis** clears a physical floor: `max(2.0 mm, 2 × the coarser in-plane spacing)` of the plane being measured." No annotation version is named, so it reads as the rule for every pin.
- **Truth:** The mm floor is the v1.4.0 rule only; every earlier (still loadable) planner version used a raw pixel count. /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:319-326 `_VERSION_NOTES`: "1.1.0": "corrected TL filtering, cluster threshold 20px", "1.0.0": "original TL filtering, cluster threshold 200px". /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:1374 `CLUSTER_FILTER_METHODS = ("major_axis", "pixel_count")` with the legacy branch at :1595-1598 (`cluster_filter_method == "pixel_count" and cluster_size < cluster_size_threshold`) and the mm branch only at :1743-1745. /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.4.0.md: "The previous rule was a raw pixel count (20 px; 10 px for LIDC-IDRI)". This is a regression: HEAD (`git show HEAD:docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md`) scoped the rule with an explicit "v1.4.0 (current)" / "v1.3.0 and earlier" bullet pair, which the fix pass collapsed into one unqualified sentence. It now also contradicts the sibling page, which presents the same formula only under "### v1.4.0: regenerated T/L annotations" (statistics.md:77-79) while stating that leaderboard results use v1.0.0 (statistics.md:8).
- **Fix:** Re-scope the sentence, e.g. "From annotation v1.4.0, a component is measured only when its fitted ellipse's major axis clears `max(2.0 mm, 2 × the coarser in-plane spacing)` …; at v1.3.0 and earlier the floor was a raw pixel count (200 px at v1.0.0, 20 px from v1.1.0, 10 px for LIDC-IDRI)."

### M8. `docsite/source/dataset/loading.md:116`
*docsite:dataset (bm) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`--tasks_json` — path to a task-list JSON; its top-level keys are read as task names (the same format used under `tasks_list/`)." — points the reader at `tasks_list/` as the source of usable task lists.
- **Truth:** Three of the six top-level files in /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/tasks_list/ (tasks_MedVision-detect-CoT.json, tasks_MedVision-TL-CoT.json, tasks_MedVision-AD-CoT.json) carry a `-CoT` suffix on every key (e.g. `CrossMoDA_BoxCoordinate_Task01_Axial-CoT`), and `tasks_to_configs` only appends the split and rewrites BoxCoordinate→BoxSize — src/medvision_bm/utils/data_utils.py:29-39 — yielding `CrossMoDA_BoxSize_Task01_Axial-CoT_Test`. No such builder config exists: `grep -c CoT /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py` returns 0, and only `CrossMoDA_BoxSize_Task01_Axial_Test` is declared (MedVision.py:5077). The repo's own skill notes this exact failure: skills/medvision/references/troubleshooting.md:42 — "`BuilderConfig '<task>-CoT_Test' not found` from `download_datasets --tasks_json` | eval task lists carry the `-CoT` suffix, which `tasks_to_configs` does not strip". Only the `__train_SFT` lists (e.g. `CrossMoDA_BoxSize_Task01_Axial`) expand to valid configs.
- **Fix:** Add the constraint: "…(the same format used under `tasks_list/`). Note the eval lists there — `tasks_MedVision-{detect,TL,AD}-CoT.json` — carry a `-CoT` suffix that is not stripped, so they expand to nonexistent configs; pass a `__train_SFT` list or a configs CSV instead."

### M9. `docsite/source/index.md:73`
*docsite:start (bm) · regression · adjusted*

- **Claim:** Quickstart step 1 comment: "The later steps read tasks_list/ and completed_tasks/, which are repo directories and are NOT shipped in the PyPI wheel." — presented as the reason a clone is required instead of `pip install medvision-bm`.
- **Truth:** `completed_tasks` is gitignored: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/.gitignore:212 lists `completed_tasks`, and `git ls-files completed_tasks` returns 0 tracked files. A fresh `git clone` therefore contains no `completed_tasks/` directory at all — it is created at runtime by `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(json_path), exist_ok=True)` in src/medvision_bm/utils/utils.py:110 (`update_task_status`). Only `tasks_list/` is a real repo directory (`git ls-files tasks_list` lists tasks_list/README.md, tasks_list/OOD/*.json, etc.). This sentence is new in the working tree (git diff of docsite/source/index.md replaces the old `# 1. Install from PyPI` block), so it is a regression introduced by the fix pass.
- **Fix:** Reword to name only the directory that actually ships, e.g. "# 1. Clone and install. The later steps read tasks_list/, a repo directory that is NOT shipped in the PyPI wheel; completed_tasks/ is created on first run."
- **Verifier adjustment:** severity: low (not medium). Only the `completed_tasks/` half of the comment is wrong: it is gitignored (.gitignore:212, 0 tracked files) and created on first run by update_task_status (src/medvision_bm/utils/utils.py:110), not a repo directory shipped or missing from the wheel. tasks_list/ is correctly described. Fix as suggested.

### M10. `docsite/source/extending/add-a-model.md:46`
*docsite:train (bm) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Section 2 says "Each model subclasses the framework's `lmms` base and must implement: `def generate_until(self, requests) -> List[str]`" and "That is the entire inference surface the harness relies on"; the checklist (line 116) likewise lists only `new class, @register_model("<key>"), generate_until()`. Following this literally produces a class that cannot be instantiated.
- **Truth:** `lmms` is an abstract base class (`class lmms(abc.ABC)` at src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/api/model.py:18) with THREE `@abc.abstractmethod`s, not one: `loglikelihood` (model.py:32-33), `generate_until` (model.py:60-61) and `generate_until_multi_round` (model.py:79-80). Python refuses to instantiate a subclass that leaves any of them undefined (`TypeError: Can't instantiate abstract class ... with abstract methods generate_until_multi_round, loglikelihood`). Every shipped wrapper therefore defines all three, the two unused ones as raise-stubs: lmms_eval/models/vllm_qwen25vl.py:374 (`def loglikelihood`) and :377 (`def generate_until_multi_round`); lmms_eval/models/gemini.py:491 and :494; lmms_eval/models/claude.py:334 and :337.
- **Fix:** In section 2 say the `lmms` base declares three abstract methods and that a new model must define all three — `generate_until()` with the real implementation, plus `loglikelihood()` and `generate_until_multi_round()` as `raise NotImplementedError` stubs (as every shipped wrapper does) — while keeping the point that `generate_until()` is the only one the MedVision `generate_until` tasks actually call. Add the two stubs to the checklist line 116.

### M11. `docsite/source/fine-tuning/sft.md:140`
*docsite:train (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The "Key hyperparameters" table's `--save_steps` / `--eval_steps` / `--logging_steps` row was widened by the fix pass to cover all launchers — "`100 / 100 / 50` (the two Qwen2.5-VL-7B LoRA reference recipes); `100 / 100 / 20` (the other 19 launchers)" — but the neighbouring rows in the same "Recipe defaults" column hold only for the three Qwen2.5-VL-7B reference recipes, so the table now reads as if all 21 launchers share those values.
- **Truth:** Read against script/sft/ (21 launchers): the widened row is correct (100/100/50 only in train__SFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__*.sh:53-55; 100/100/20 in the other 19). But `--use_flash_attention_2 true` is `false` in 12 launchers — all six Gemma-4-31B and all six Qwen3.5/Qwen3.6 scripts, e.g. script/sft/train__SFT-CoT__Gemma-4-31B-it__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:78 `use_flash_attention_2=false` and script/sft/train__fullSFT-CoT__Qwen3.6-27B__D110k-AD5.5k-TL5.5k__512x512.sh:72,98. `--per_device_train_batch_size` "LoRA `4`, full-FT `8`" and `--gradient_accumulation_steps` `8` hold only in the three Qwen2.5VL7B scripts (…__512x512.sh:94-96); elsewhere batch is 1 or 2 and accumulation 16/32/64 (Gemma-4-31B LoRA:124,126 = 1 / 32; Qwen3.6 fullSFT:107,109 = 1 / 64). `--save_total_limit` `10` holds in only 5 of 21 (Gemma/MedGemma LoRA use 5, e.g. Gemma-4-31B LoRA:76; every 27B/31B fullSFT uses 3, e.g. Qwen3.6 fullSFT:66).
- **Fix:** Either scope the whole table explicitly ("defaults in the three Qwen2.5-VL-7B reference recipes") and drop the "other 19 launchers" clause from the cadence row, or add the per-family values to the other rows (batch 1-2 / accumulation 16-64 / `save_total_limit` 3-5 / `use_flash_attention_2 false` for the Gemma-4-31B and Qwen3.5-3.6 scripts).

### M12. `docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md:9`
*ds:filters-annotation (ds) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "…**25.7 million single-instance annotations**, or **50.5 million multi-instance annotations** … Neither figure counts instances: several boxes or clusters of the same target on one slice are one annotation in both." Restated at line 66 ("A benchmark sample is a *(2D slice, target)* pair, counted **per target, not per instance**") and line 86 ("the multi-instance set contains every *recorded* sample — not every mask component").
- **Truth:** True for Box, false for T/L. The script that produces every number on statistics.md counts T/L multi-instance PER ELLIPSE: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/src/medvision_bm/utils/summarize_datasets.py:255 "T/L raw — every ellipse (``sum(len(slice_profile))``), no single-cluster filter" and :272 `n_tl_r += len(sp)`, where `sp` is the per-slice list of fitted ellipses (one entry per measured cluster, built at /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:2493-2531). Contrast Box at summarize_datasets.py:234 `n_r += 1 # raw: one per (slice, label)` with the explicit docstring ":208-210 a label split into N connected components still counts once, not N". Cross-check: every multi-instance T/L cell in statistics.md's v1.4.0 table equals the per-CLUSTER landmark total of /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.4.0.md:52-64 exactly (BraTS24 1,645,550; MSWAL 145,136; MSD 712,247; total 3,801,540 = "Measured" clusters at release-v1.4.0.md:96). The loader itself yields one row per slice for T/L (/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:12450-12467, biometric_profile is a Sequence of measurement blocks), so 50.5M is not a row count either.
- **Fix:** Qualify the sentence: per-target counting holds for Box (one annotation per (slice, label) however many components) and for A/D, but the multi-instance **T/L** figure counts every measured ellipse on the slice — which is why 3,801,540 is both the v1.4.0 landmark total and the multi-instance T/L total. Either say so, or relabel the T/L multi-instance column as a measurement count.

### M13. `docsite/source/dataset/statistics.md:79`
*ds:filters-annotation (ds) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "**v1.4.0 regenerates the Tumor-Lesion-Size annotations of all 12 T/L datasets.** Clusters are selected by a physical size floor in millimetres … and the ellipse fit is guarded against degenerate results … Published T/L landmarks grow from **75,840 to 3,801,540 (50×)**" — i.e. the 50× growth is attributed to the mm floor plus the fit guards.
- **Truth:** v1.4.0 made THREE annotation-procedure changes, and the omitted one is the dominant cause. /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.4.0.md:14 lists "`all_within` containment gate removed" as a separate change, and :24 states outright: "This removal, **not the lower floor**, accounts for most of the growth: on KiPA22 the median size moved only from 34.3 mm to 33.5 mm while the maximum *increased* from 85.3 mm to 106.8 mm, which a lowered floor cannot do." The removal is implemented at /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:1758-1779 ("NO quality gate on the fitted ellipse. Both halves of the historical `all_within` check are gone"). Neither this page nor concepts.md mentions it anywhere (`grep -rn 'all_within\|containment' docsite/source` → 0 hits).
- **Fix:** Add the third change to the v1.4.0 paragraph: "…the `all_within` containment gate — which rejected rotated ellipses whose axis endpoints fell outside a scaled bounding box — is removed, and (per the release note) this removal, not the lower floor, accounts for most of the growth."

### M14. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:15`
*ds:filters-annotation (ds) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Under the heading "**Annotation generation** (the current **v1.4.0** rules)": "*Bounding box:* fitted per binary-mask cluster on each 2D slice; clusters <10 px in any dimension excluded."
- **Truth:** Annotation generation applies NO size test: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:671-730 (`_find_bounding_boxes_2D`) records a box for every connected component with no threshold. The <10 px rule is a LOAD-TIME single-instance filter in the loader, /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:12299-12303 `elif not disable_sample_filtering and (case["bounding_boxes"][0]["dimensions"][0] < 10 or …[1] < 10): continue`, and it is bypassed entirely by `MedVision_DISABLE_SAMPLE_FILTERING=true` (MedVision.py:12236-12239). It also does not "exclude a cluster": it drops the whole (slice, target) SAMPLE, and it can only ever fire on a lone box because the `len(case["bounding_boxes"]) > 1` test at MedVision.py:12296 runs first. (The paper says the same thing at paper.tex:140, so this is paper text, but the skill presents it as "the current v1.4.0 rules".)
- **Fix:** Move the rule out of the generation bullet: "*Bounding box:* fitted per binary-mask cluster on each 2D slice, with no size test at generation time. The single-instance loader filter then drops a slice-sample when it holds more than one box, or when its only box is <10 px on either side."

### M15. `skills/medvision/references/concepts-and-glossary.md:29`
*ds:filters-annotation (ds) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Detection row, "Ground truth source" column: "box fitted to each mask cluster on a 2D slice (clusters < 10 px on a side excluded; single-instance filter drops slices with > 1 box)" — the parenthesis attributes the <10 px rule to GT generation and only the >1-box rule to the single-instance filter.
- **Truth:** Both are the same load-time filter and both are gated by `MedVision_DISABLE_SAMPLE_FILTERING`: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:12294-12303 tests `len(case["bounding_boxes"]) > 1` first, then `dimensions[0] < 10 or dimensions[1] < 10` on the surviving single box. The generator (benchmark_planner.py:671-730) writes a box for every connected component with no size test. Consequence the current wording hides: a slice with one large cluster plus one tiny cluster is dropped by the >1-box rule, not served as a single-box sample with the small cluster "excluded". docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md:73 states this correctly, so the two documents disagree.
- **Fix:** Rewrite the parenthesis as "single-instance filter drops a slice-sample with > 1 box, or whose only box is < 10 px on either side", and leave the GT column as "box fitted to each mask cluster on a 2D slice".

### M16. `docsite/source/dataset/loading.md:158`
*ds:loader-env (ds) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Note that the CLI's `--force_download_data` maps to `MedVision_FORCE_DOWNLOAD_DATA`; it forces the builder to run and re-fetch raw data for every requested item."
- **Truth:** The flag only sets an env var; it cannot force the builder to run. `src/medvision_bm/benchmark/download_datasets.py:54-58` calls `setup_env_hf_medvision_ds(..., force_download_data=args.force_download_data)`, which at `src/medvision_bm/utils/install_utils.py:206-208` merely sets `MedVision_FORCE_DOWNLOAD_DATA="true"`. The actual load, `src/medvision_bm/utils/data_utils.py:61-67`, calls `load_dataset(..., trust_remote_code=True, split=split, streaming=False)` with **no** `download_mode`, so a valid Arrow cache short-circuits the script. `MedVision.py:11749` reads the env var only inside `_split_generators`, which a warm cache never reaches. This contradicts the same page at lines 136-139 ("both raw-data controls are only consulted **while the builder runs**... To refresh raw data: `download_mode=\"force_redownload\"` **and** ...") — and the CLI exposes no way to set `download_mode`.
- **Fix:** Rewrite as: "the CLI's `--force_download_data` only sets `MedVision_FORCE_DOWNLOAD_DATA=true`. It does not pass `download_mode`, so for any config that already has a valid Arrow cache the builder is skipped and the flag has no effect; it re-fetches raw data only for configs that are being built (or rebuilt) anyway."

### M17. `docsite/source/dataset/concepts.md:79`
*ds:loader-env (ds) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The T/L generation-time note now states the mm floor unconditionally: "A component is measured only when its fitted ellipse's **major axis** clears a physical floor: `max(2.0 mm, 2 × the coarser in-plane spacing)` ... The ellipse fit itself is additionally guarded against degenerate results (a contour under 5 points, ... a major axis over 1.5× the cluster's own bounding-box diagonal are all rejected)." The previous per-version breakdown (v1.4.0 vs v1.3.0-and-earlier) was deleted in the current working-tree edit.
- **Truth:** The mm floor and the four fit guards exist only in the v1.4.0 planner path. `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:1552-1558` documents two selectable modes — `"pixel_count"` (legacy): "drop a cluster whose PIXEL COUNT is below `cluster_size_threshold`, before any fitting" (applied at line 1594-1597) — and `"major_axis"` (default): `floor_mm = max(min_major_axis_mm, 2.0 * max(pixel_sizes))` (line 1743). `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.4.0.md:31`: "The previous rule was a raw pixel count (20 px; 10 px for LIDC-IDRI)", and `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.1.0.md:96` records `CLUSTER_SIZE_THRESHOLD` going from 200 to 20 px at v1.1.0. The containment (`all_within`) gate that v1.4.0 removed is described at `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.1.0.md:205`. The same page (concepts.md:74) and loading.md:41-58 still document loading pins 1.0.0-1.3.0, so the unconditional wording is wrong for every non-latest pin.
- **Fix:** Restore a one-line version qualifier, corrected: "**v1.4.0 (current)** — major-axis floor `max(2.0 mm, 2 × the coarser in-plane spacing)` plus the four degenerate-fit guards. **v1.1.0-v1.3.0** — a raw pixel-count floor of 20 px (10 px for LIDC-IDRI) plus a containment gate that discarded rotated ellipses. **v1.0.0** — the same pixel-count rule with a 200 px floor."

### M18. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/dataset-and-tasks/references/downloading.md:64`
*ds:loader-env (ds) · wrong-version · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Version switches do not need any of this: since 1.1.1 each resolved version (and each data root) has its own cache id, and a downgrade reuses on-disk data (annotation zips are cumulative)."
- **Truth:** v1.1.1 added version isolation keyed on the **requested** version only: `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.1.1.md:135` — "`MedVision.py` config default `version=\"1.1.1\"` ... folded into the HF builder cache fingerprint via `create_config_id`". Both the *resolved*-version key and the data-root token landed in **v1.2.0**: `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/doc/release-v1.2.0.md:77` ("The HuggingFace builder-cache fingerprint was derived from the value of `MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION`, not from the `benchmark_plan_{kind}_v{X}.json.gz` that value resolved to") and `:209` ("The canonicalised data root is now part of the builder-cache fingerprint"). Current code confirms both: `MedVision.py:1183-1201` resolves the pin before building the token and folds in `hashlib.sha1(_data_root(strict=False)...)[:8]`. Consequently the blanket "version switches do not need any of this" is unsafe for caches built before v1.2.0 — `/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/README.md:642` requires clearing those once with lever [1] AND [2].
- **Fix:** Change to: "since **1.2.0** each *resolved* version and each data root has its own cache id ... . One historical exception: a `Tumor-Lesion-Size` cache built before v1.2.0 (keyed on the *requested* version) can hold stale rows — clear it once with `download_mode=\"force_redownload\"` **and** `MedVision_FORCE_DOWNLOAD_DATA=True`."

### M19. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:12`
*paper:dataset (paper) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Train/test split is **70/30 at the subject level**."
- **Truth:** paper.tex:600 (Table appendix:tab:dataset caption) is the only split statement in the paper: "Every dataset is partitioned into training and test sets at a 7:3 ratio." It never claims a subject-level partition. The code splits at the 3D-image-file level, not the subject level: /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/src/medvision_ds/utils/benchmark_planner.py:104-111 globs `*.nii.gz` in the dataset folder, `random.shuffle(file_list)`, `split_idx = int(len(file_list) * self.split_ratio)` (split_ratio=0.7 at :59). Several datasets store multiple volumes per subject, so a subject can land on both sides: ACDC = 300 volumes for 150 patients (per-frame files, download_raw.py:60 keeps `patient*` frame volumes; paper table lists 300 3D images), CAMUS = 1,000 volumes for 500 patients (2CH/4CH half-sequences kept, only the `_ED`/`_ES` 2D files are deleted — CAMUS/download_raw.py:61-66; paper table lists 1,000 3D images).
- **Fix:** Say "70/30 (7:3) per dataset, split over 3D image volumes" and drop "at the subject level" (or note explicitly that the split is per volume, so datasets with several volumes per subject are not subject-disjoint).

### M20. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:12`
*paper:dataset (paper) · coverage-gap · adjusted*

- **Claim:** The Dataset block gives a single conflated count ("image-annotation pairs") and carries none of the paper's other headline scale facts.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:600 defines the two totals the skill collapses: "A benchmark sample is a (2D slice, target) pair. **Single-instance** annotations retain a target only when it appears as a single, sufficiently large instance on the slice; these are the annotations used for benchmarking and fine-tuning. **Multi-instance** annotations retain every annotated target regardless of how many instances it has." paper.tex:91/:101 also give 29.0K 3D images and 11.2M annotated 2D slices, and paper.tex:632 gives per-task single-instance totals (Box 24,236,327; T/L 32,249; A/D 7,925). A README/webpage writer using this skill cannot reproduce any of those.
- **Fix:** Add the 3D-image and 2D-slice counts, and state the single- vs multi-instance distinction (single-instance = the benchmarking/fine-tuning set, 24.3M; multi-instance = 45.3M) plus the per-task Box/T/L/A/D totals.
- **Verifier adjustment:** Residual coverage gap (severity low, subsumed in part by finding 0): after adding the 29.0K/11.2M/24.3M/45.3M triple, the skill still lacks the paper's single- vs multi-instance definition (paper.tex:600 — single-instance keeps a target only when it appears as a single sufficiently large instance and is the set used for benchmarking and fine-tuning) and the per-task single-instance totals (Box 24,236,327; T/L 32,249; A/D 7,925, paper.tex:632).

### M21. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:methods (paper) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "SR (success rate) = fraction of outputs with the required numeric values parseable from `<answer></answer>`."
- **Truth:** paper.tex:198: "a rule-based parser reads the <answer></answer> tags, and an LLM-assisted parsing pipeline additionally recovers answers stated outside this format ... All reported metrics are computed from the LLM-parsed responses. Success rate (SR) is calculated as the proportion of successfully parsed outputs among all samples." paper.tex:~772 (LLM-Assisted Response Parsing): "parsing success rises from 92.2% to 98.6% on detection, from 81.7% to 94.5% on T/L ... 79.0% to 93.9% on A/D" - the reported SR explicitly counts the "answer in another format" outcome, i.e. answers NOT in <answer> tags. The skill's own last sentence on the same line ("Reported numbers are computed from the LLM-judge re-parsed records") contradicts its SR definition.
- **Fix:** Define SR as the paper does: the fraction of samples from which the required number of values could be parsed, counting both rule-parsed <answer> outputs and LLM-recovered answers stated in another format; note the rule-only SR is the strict-regex baseline (92.2/81.7/79.0% pooled) vs LLM-parsed (98.6/94.5/93.9%).

### M22. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:methods (paper) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Plain MAE/MRE/IoU are computed **only on successfully parsed samples**"
- **Truth:** True of MAE/MRE, but NOT of detection IoU/F1/P/R in the shipped code: src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:499-523 sets `mean_absolute_error = np.nan` but `IoU = 0; f1 = 0; precision = 0; recall = 0` on a parse failure (comment: "return 0 for overlap metrics instead of NaN ... This ensures failed predictions are counted in averages (0% performance) rather than excluded"). src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_detection_task.py:148-181 then only skips non-finite values, so the zeros enter `sum_IoU`/`count_valid_IoU` and the mean IoU/F1/P/R denominators include failed samples. The paper (paper.tex:198) makes the same claim as the skill, so the skill is repeating an assertion the code does not implement.
- **Fix:** Qualify: "MAE/MRE exclude unparsed samples (NaN); detection IoU/F1/P/R score a failed parse as 0 and therefore average over ALL samples (parse_utils.py:499-523) - a known divergence from the paper's wording."

### M23. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:21`
*paper:methods (paper) · contradicts-paper · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Real-world units: annotations are in **mm** (and degrees for angles), NOT pixels. The benchmark conveys pixel size in the text prompt" - stated without exception for any task.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:838 (Details of SFT with CoT, Prompt Structure): "For the T/L size and A/D measurement tasks, the additional information provides the image size in pixels and the physical pixel size ...; **the detection prompt omits this part because its answer is expressed in relative coordinates**." paper.tex:195 likewise adds spacing info only "for T/L and A/D tasks", and the detection format requirement (Box box:cot_detection) says "Use relative coordinates in the image space ... values between 0 and 1". So one of the three task families has neither mm targets nor pixel size in the prompt.
- **Fix:** Scope the sentence to T/L and A/D: "T/L and A/D targets are in mm (degrees for angles) and their prompts carry image size + physical pixel size; detection targets are relative coordinates in [0,1] and the detection prompt omits the spacing block."

### M24. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:methods (paper) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The Metrics block enumerates SR, detection region metrics + Acc@IoU sweep, and MAE/MRE/MRE<0.1 as the benchmark's reported metrics.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:525-526 is a main-body subsection \subsection{Clinical Decision Agreement}: predicted measurements are mapped to clinical categories through published cutoff tables (Steiner SNA/SNB bands; AJCC kidney T-staging) and agreement is scored with "chance-corrected, quadratic-weighted Cohen's kappa_w", with citable headline numbers ("Off-the-shelf VLMs reach at most fair agreement (kappa_w<=0.361), whereas MedVision-V0 attains ... 0.332 / 0.439 / 0.726"). paper.tex:1418-1429 gives the protocol (95% percentile bootstrap over whole imaging volumes, 4,000 resamples; one-sided p-value). The skill never mentions CDA.
- **Fix:** Add one line to the Metrics block: "CDA (clinical decision agreement): measurement -> clinical category via published cutoffs (SNA 82+-2 deg, SNB 80+-2 deg, AJCC T1a/T1b/T2a/T2b at 40/70/100 mm), scored with quadratic-weighted Cohen's kappa_w, bootstrapped over whole volumes (4,000 resamples). V0: 0.332 SNA / 0.439 SNB / 0.726 kidney staging; all off-the-shelf VLMs <= 0.361."

### M25. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:26`
*paper:methods (paper) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** SFT: "Weighted random sampler oversamples minority tasks." - no temperature given, while line 31 attributes "T=8 task sampling" to the multi-task RFT ablation.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:1043 (Temperature-Scaled Task Mixing, Instantiations in SFT and RFT): "The SFT stage uses T=5, giving task probabilities of approximately 47.7% for detection and 26.2% each for T/L and A/D (natural proportions: 90.9%, 4.5%, and 4.5%). The multi-task RFT ablation uses T=8, giving approximately 42.1% for detection and 29.0% each for T/L and A/D." With only T=8 stated, a reader reproducing the SFT sampler is left to assume the wrong temperature.
- **Fix:** In the SFT bullet write "weighted random sampler with temperature-scaled task mixing, P(t) ∝ N_t^(1/T), T=5 -> ~47.7% detection / 26.2% T/L / 26.2% A/D (natural 90.9/4.5/4.5), sampled with replacement"; keep T=8 (~42.1/29.0/29.0) on the multi-task RFT ablation.

### M26. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:metrics-results (paper) · stale-behavior · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "Plain MAE/MRE/IoU are computed **only on successfully parsed samples**" — stated as a blanket rule covering the detection region metrics.
- **Truth:** True for measurement (summarize_TL_task.py:262-271 divides sum_MAE/sum_MRE by count_valid_AE/count_valid_RE), but false for detection: parse_utils.py:497-523 returns IoU/F1/Precision/Recall = 0 (NOT NaN) on a parse failure — "Invalid prediction format: return 0 for overlap metrics instead of NaN / This ensures failed predictions are counted in averages (0% performance)" — and summarize_detection_task.py:149-181 admits every finite value into the running sum, so detection IoU/F1/P/R average over ALL samples; only avgMAE is NaN-excluded. summarize_detection_task.py:155-157 says the same for the sweep: "failed parses give iou=0 -> miss at every threshold". The paper's Metric Definition makes the same blanket statement (paper.tex:819, "the detection and measurement metrics are calculated only on the successfully parsed predictions"), so the skill inherits the paper's imprecision rather than contradicting it — but a reader recomputing a published detection IoU on parsed-only samples will not reproduce the number for any model with SR < 100 (e.g. Llama3.2-Vision detection SR 89.4, paper.tex:363).
- **Fix:** Add the caveat: "(in code, only avgMAE excludes failures; detection IoU/F1/P/R score a parse failure as 0, so their denominator is every sample — the paper's blanket statement at Metric Definition is loose here)".
- **Verifier adjustment:** category: imprecise-vs-code (not stale-behavior); severity: low-medium. Corrected evidence paths: src/medvision_bm/utils/parse_utils.py:497-523 and src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_detection_task.py:149-181, src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_TL_task.py:262-271. Fix as suggested: note that in code only avgMAE excludes failures, while detection IoU/F1/P/R score a parse failure as 0 over all samples.

### M27. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:23`
*paper:metrics-results (paper) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The Metrics block enumerates SR, detection region metrics, the IoU sweep and MAE/MRE/MRE<0.1, and nothing else — the paper's clinical-decision-agreement (CDA) metric family is absent from the whole file.
- **Truth:** paper.tex:526 (main text \subsection{Clinical Decision Agreement}) and paper.tex:1407-1424 (\section{Clinical Decision Agreement Analysis}) define a second reported metric family: predicted measurement -> clinical category via a published cutoff table (Steiner SNA 82±2 deg, SNB 80±2 deg; AJCC T1a <=40 mm, T1b >40-70, T2a >70-100, T2b >100 mm), scored with Acc, Cohen's kappa and — the primary statistic — quadratic-weighted kappa_w, with a percentile bootstrap over whole imaging volumes (4,000 resamples) and interpretation bands 0.21-0.40 fair / 0.41-0.60 moderate / 0.61-0.80 substantial (paper.tex:1422). Headline numbers (paper.tex:526, Table tab:cda_agreement at paper.tex:1438+): MedVision-V0 kappa_w = 0.332 (SNA), 0.439 (SNB), 0.726 (kidney staging); every off-the-shelf model kappa_w <= 0.361.
- **Fix:** Add one line to the Metrics block: "CDA (clinical decision agreement): measurement -> clinical category through a published cutoff table (Steiner SNA/SNB norm bands; AJCC kidney T-staging 40/70/100 mm), scored by quadratic-weighted Cohen's kappa_w with a 4,000-resample bootstrap over whole volumes; V0 = 0.332 SNA / 0.439 SNB / 0.726 staging vs <=0.361 for every off-the-shelf model. No re-inference — it re-scores the parsed outputs."

### M28. `skills/medvision-paper/SKILL.md:32`
*paper:metrics-results (paper) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Result: MedVision-V0 outperforms all 17 evaluated off-the-shelf VLMs across all three tasks, and a segmentation specialist (BiomedParse) on detection + T/L. SFT gives the large jump; RFT adds consistent gains and better OOD generalization" — the only result statement in the file, and it carries no numbers at all.
- **Truth:** The claim itself is supported (paper.tex:348, 367, 402, 435, 454; BiomedParse Table tab:biomedparse_comparison, paper.tex:500-514; SFT/RFT progression Table tab:sft_rft_ablation, paper.tex:1223-1249). But the citable v1.0.0 numbers a README/webpage writer must match are absent: anatomy detection F1 79.1 / IoU 72.0 and T/L detection F1 46.9 / IoU 38.2 (paper.tex:348, 367); T/L size MAE 10.5 mm, MRE 26.0% (paper.tex:402); distance MAE 3.6 mm, MRE 6.4% (paper.tex:435); angle MAE 4.7 deg, MRE 52.1% (paper.tex:454); OOD drops anatomy F1 79.1 -> 49.5 (plane) / 36.0 (target), T/L det F1 46.9 -> 36.6 / 45.5, T/L MRE 26.0 -> 36.4 / 34.7 (paper.tex:489). The skill's own description says it exists so that "scale numbers matter" text can be written from it.
- **Fix:** Add a "Headline numbers (v1.0.0 annotations, LLM-parsed)" line with det F1 79.1 / 46.9, T/L MRE 26.0 (MAE 10.5 mm), distance MRE 6.4 (MAE 3.6 mm), angle MRE 52.1 (MAE 4.7 deg), and the three OOD deltas — flagged as as-published, matching the existing v1.0.0 caveat at line 19.

### M30. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/benchmark-evaluation/references/model-catalog.md:89`
*skills:bench-eval (bm) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "HF data-parallel (`medgemma`, `lingshu`, `meddr`, `huatuogpt_vision`, `llava_med`, `healthgpt`): … `batch_size` is effectively cosmetic — every one of these wrappers generates one sample per iteration."
- **Truth:** Two of the six genuinely batch. lmms_eval/models/medgemma.py:211 `outputs = self.pipe(text=batch_messages, max_new_tokens=_max_new_tokens, batch_size=self.batch_size)`; lmms_eval/models/lingshu.py:172-205 builds one padded batch (`self._processor(text=batch_texts, ..., padding=True)`) and calls `self._model.generate(**inputs, ...)` once for the whole batch. The sibling page states this correctly: references/hardware-and-parallelism.md:70-73 "batch_size is cosmetic for four of the six … MedGemma and Lingshu genuinely batch (… medgemma.py:211 …)".
- **Fix:** Change to "`batch_size` is cosmetic for four of the six (MedDr, HuatuoGPT-Vision, LLaVA-Med, HealthGPT — one sample per iteration); MedGemma and Lingshu genuinely batch", matching hardware-and-parallelism.md:70-73.

### M31. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/requirements-catalog.md:68`
*skills:data-env (bm) · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** In the "Model key → wrapper → extra → automatic install parameters" table the **torch step** column is `-` for `healthgpt` (line 68), `huatuogpt_vision` (69), `lingshu` (71), `llavamed` (74) and `meddr` (75) — i.e. those wrappers install no torch when run without `--skip_env_setup`.
- **Truth:** All five call a flash-attention helper that force-reinstalls torch: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__healthgpt.py:333 and eval__huatuogpt_vision.py:274 and eval__lingshu.py:202 call `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py311_v2()`; eval__llava_med.py:273 calls `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py310_v2()`; eval__meddr.py:288 calls `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py39_v2()`. Each of those helpers starts with `install_torch_cu124()` (src/medvision_bm/utils/install_utils.py:472, 545, 618) which runs `pip install torch==2.6.0+cu124 torchvision==0.21.0+cu124 torchaudio==2.6.0+cu124 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu124 --force-reinstall` (install_utils.py:397-417), then installs the cp39/cp310/cp311 flash_attn 2.7.3 wheel plus `numpy==1.26.4` and `protobuf==3.20`. The same file already states at line 3 that exactly these five eval sets pin a flash-attn wheel, so the table contradicts its own intro.
- **Fix:** Replace the `-` in the torch-step cell of those five rows with the helper actually called — `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py311_v2` (healthgpt, huatuogpt_vision, lingshu), `..._py310_v2` (llavamed), `..._py39_v2` (meddr) — and note it installs torch/torchvision/torchaudio 2.6.0+cu124 with `--force-reinstall`, the matching flash_attn 2.7.3 wheel, numpy 1.26.4 and protobuf 3.20.

### M32. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/cli-reference.md:171`
*skills:data-env (bm) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "Every `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.eval__<model>` wrapper accepts `--skip_env_setup` (skip all installs; still sets the env vars and, for vLLM models, `setup_env_vllm`) ..." — the same claim appears at installation.md:109 ("`--skip_env_setup` skips every install but still exports the env vars and vLLM settings").
- **Truth:** Two wrappers run installs outside the `if not args.skip_env_setup:` block, so `--skip_env_setup` does not skip them. src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__healthgpt.py:332-333 — `model_configs = install_healthgpt_dependencies_post(dir_third_party, model_choice)` and `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py311_v2()` are at 4-space indent, after the `else:` branch closes at line 331; the second call force-reinstalls torch 2.6.0+cu124 (install_utils.py:618 → :397). src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__huatuogpt_vision.py:284 — `install_huatuogpt_vision_dependencies_post(dir_third_party)` is likewise outside the guard and runs `pip install transformers==4.40.0` (eval__huatuogpt_vision.py:49) plus a third-party git clone. By contrast eval__llava_med.py:272 and eval__lingshu.py:202 keep their post-install inside the guard (8-space indent), so the exception is specific to those two wrappers.
- **Fix:** Qualify both sentences: `--skip_env_setup` skips the guarded install block, but `eval__healthgpt` still runs `install_healthgpt_dependencies_post` + `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py311_v2()` (torch 2.6.0+cu124 force-reinstall) and `eval__huatuogpt_vision` still runs `install_huatuogpt_vision_dependencies_post` (`transformers==4.40.0` + third-party clone) unconditionally — so a carefully pinned env is still mutated by those two.

### M33. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/installation.md:107`
*skills:data-env (bm) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "API wrappers (`eval__claude`, `eval__gemini`, `eval__openai`, `eval__kimi`) stop after `install_medvision_ds`; HF-inference wrappers (`eval__medgemma`, `eval__healthgpt`, ...) skip torch/vLLM."
- **Truth:** True for `eval__medgemma` (src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__medgemma.py:198-200 installs only hub 0.36.0, the vendored engine and medvision_ds) but false for `eval__healthgpt`: eval__healthgpt.py:333 calls `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py311_v2()`, whose first statement is `install_torch_cu124()` (src/medvision_bm/utils/install_utils.py:618 → :397-417, torch/torchvision/torchaudio 2.6.0+cu124 with `--force-reinstall`). The same is true of the other three non-vLLM wrappers the sentence's "..." would cover — `eval__huatuogpt_vision` (:274), `eval__lingshu` (:202), `eval__llava_med` (:273), `eval__meddr` (:288). Only vLLM is genuinely skipped by all of them (`grep -n 'install_vllm(' eval__{healthgpt,huatuogpt_vision,lingshu,llava_med,meddr}.py` returns nothing).
- **Fix:** Reword to: "HF-inference wrappers skip vLLM; `eval__medgemma` installs no torch either, but `eval__healthgpt`, `eval__huatuogpt_vision`, `eval__lingshu`, `eval__llava_med` and `eval__meddr` install torch 2.6.0+cu124 plus a cp39/cp310/cp311 flash-attn wheel through `install_flash_attention_torch_and_deps_py3XX_v2()`."

### M34. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/environment-variables.md:41`
*skills:data-env (bm) · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The `HF_TOKEN` row of the "Hugging Face variables" table is a 3-column row that ends with the copyable command `export HF_TOKEN="$(printf '%s' "$HF_TOKEN" | tr -d '[:space:]')"`.
- **Truth:** The `|` inside the inline code span is not escaped, so GFM splits the cell: the row parses as 4 cells against a 3-cell header (verified by re-splitting every table row on unescaped pipes across both audited directories — only this row and environment-setup/references/troubleshooting.md:23 are over-wide). The rendered cell therefore stops at `export HF_TOKEN="$(printf '%s' "$HF_TOKEN"` and the rest lands in a phantom column, so a reader copies a truncated, unbalanced command. The sibling file gets this right: dataset-and-tasks/references/troubleshooting.md:38 writes the identical command with `\|`.
- **Fix:** Escape the pipe as `\|` inside the code span, matching dataset-and-tasks/references/troubleshooting.md:38.

### M35. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/environment-setup/references/troubleshooting.md:23`
*skills:data-env (bm) · other · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The `401`/`403`/`Illegal header value` row of the failure matrix is a 4-column row whose Fix cell gives `export HF_TOKEN="$(printf '%s' "$HF_TOKEN" | tr -d '[:space:]')"`.
- **Truth:** Same unescaped `|` inside an inline code span: the row parses as 5 cells against a 4-cell header (checked by splitting every table row on unescaped pipes; this and environment-variables.md:41 are the only two over-wide rows in the two audited directories). The rendered Fix cell is cut at `export HF_TOKEN="$(printf '%s' "$HF_TOKEN"`, and the remaining cells ("Stop when") shift one column right. dataset-and-tasks/references/troubleshooting.md:38 writes the same command correctly with `\|`.
- **Fix:** Escape the pipe as `\|` inside the code span.

### M36. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/dataset-and-tasks/references/data-layout.md:10`
*skills:data-env (bm) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** In the `Data/` tree: "`tmp_*/` # combined/filtered/formatted intermediate datasets (SFT prep)".
- **Truth:** No code creates a `tmp_*` directory under the data root. `grep -rn 'tmp_' src/medvision_bm --include=*.py` outside the vendored engine returns only `src/medvision_bm/sft/sft_utils.py:861` (`tmp_prepared_png`, which is inside `Datasets/<ds>/Images/` and is already listed separately at data-layout.md:18) and the atomic-write temp names at sft_utils.py:892 and utils/utils.py:27; `git log -S tmp_combined -- src/` and `git grep -n 'tmp_combined\|tmp_filtered\|tmp_formatted' HEAD` return nothing. The real SFT-prep default is `os.path.join(data_dir, "SFT-CoT_datasets", model_family_name, f"ds__AD{..}_D{..}_TL{..}_all{..}")` (src/medvision_bm/sft/train__SFT-CoT__qwen2_5_vl.py:186-198). The live data root matches: `ls -d /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/Github/MedVision/Data/*/` → `Datasets/ SFT-CoT_datasets/ raw_parquet/ src/ verl_datasets/`, no `tmp_*`. The only surviving `tmp_prepared_ds` mentions are stale comments in two launchers (script/sft/train__SFT-CoT__Qwen2.5VL7B__*.sh:184), which the previous audit already flagged as a repo defect.
- **Fix:** Replace the `tmp_*/` line with the directories that actually appear: `SFT-CoT_datasets/<model_family>/ds__AD<n>_D<n>_TL<n>_all<n>/` (SFT prepared datasets, train__SFT-CoT__*.py:186-198), plus `raw_parquet/` and `verl_datasets/` as the conventional `--parquet_ds_dir` targets used by script/dataset/build_visualize_parquet_ds.sh:17 and the RFT builders.

### M37. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/dataset-and-tasks/references/data-layout.md:59`
*skills:data-env (bm) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** One schema is given for all biometry plans: `slice entry (biometry) = {"slice_idx", "n_total_clusters", "slice_profile": [[{"metric_type","metric_map_name","metric_key","metric_value","metric_unit","slice_dim"}, ...]]}`.
- **Truth:** That shape is the `fromSeg` (T/L) family only. Verified on the shipped plans: Data/Datasets/KiPA22/benchmark_plan_biometry_v1.1.1.json.gz → entry keys `['n_total_clusters','slice_idx','slice_profile']` with `slice_profile` a list of lists. Data/Datasets/Ceph-Biometrics-400/benchmark_plan_biometry_v1.0.0.json.gz (landmark family) → entry keys `['slice_idx','slice_profile']` with a FLAT list: `{"slice_idx": 0, "slice_profile": [{"metric_type": "angle", "metric_map_name": "angles_map", ...}]}` — no `n_total_clusters`. The loader relies on that split: MedVision.py:12442-12450 reads `case["n_total_clusters"]` only in the Tumor-Lesion-Size branch. The two families are named in this skill's own concepts.md:81, and the task-key list at data-layout.md:44-46 likewise mixes landmark-only keys (`landmarks_map`, `lines_map`, `angles_map`) with fromSeg-only keys (`target_label`, `cluster_size_threshold`, `min_major_axis_mm`) — Ceph-Biometrics-400's task dict has none of the latter three.
- **Fix:** Split the biometry line into two: landmark family (AFIDs, Ceph-Biometrics-400, FeTA24, PDDCA, VerSe) = `{"slice_idx", "slice_profile": [{metric_type, metric_map_name, metric_key, metric_value, metric_unit, slice_dim}, ...]}`; fromSeg / T-L family = the existing shape with `n_total_clusters` (v1.1.0+) and the doubly-nested `slice_profile`. Mark `target_label` / `cluster_size_threshold` / `min_major_axis_mm` as fromSeg-only in the task-key list.

### M40. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/biomedparse-ablation/SKILL.md:99`
*skills:extend-ablation (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Safe operating rule 1 reads: "Inference (`run_inference.py`) and fine-tuning (`finetune.py`) **require a CUDA GPU**, and `setup.sh` additionally needs `nvcc` for the detectron2 build; the prepare and eval steps import no torch and run on CPU, `nvcc` / for the detectron2 build, network access to GitHub and HuggingFace, and a populated MedVision data directory." — the trailing clause makes the prepare/eval steps appear to require nvcc and GitHub access.
- **Truth:** The sentence is a botched merge: the fragment "`nvcc` for the detectron2 build, network access to GitHub and HuggingFace, and a populated MedVision data directory" is a leftover from the setup.sh requirement list and now dangles off the prepare/eval clause. The correct split is stated in the sibling file references/setup.md:3-4 ("Everything here ... **requires** conda, a CUDA 12.4 GPU with `nvcc` (detectron2 is compiled from source), network access to GitHub and HuggingFace, and a MedVision data directory"). The prepare/eval scripts genuinely import no torch and no build tooling: script/ablation/biomedparse/src/eval_detect.py:1-25, src/eval_tl.py:1-21, src/prepare_test_data_detect.py:1-25 (grep for `import torch` over all five prepare/eval scripts returns nothing); they do need the conda env, the HF cache/network and the data dir (src/eval_detect.py:142-165 reloads every `*_Test` config).
- **Fix:** Rewrite as one sentence, e.g.: "Inference (`run_inference.py`) and fine-tuning (`finetune.py`) **require a CUDA GPU**; `setup.sh` additionally needs `nvcc` (detectron2 is built from source) and network access to GitHub and HuggingFace. The prepare and eval steps import no torch and run on CPU, but still need the conda env, HuggingFace access and a populated MedVision data directory."

### M41. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/extending-models-and-tasks/references/image-size-dispatch.md:14`
*skills:extend-ablation (bm) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "It is called from every `create_doc_to_text_*` factory (T/L, A/D, detection, MaskSize, and their CoT / visual-prompt / scaledPS variants)." — i.e. detection prompt builders call `get_resized_img_shape()`.
- **Truth:** No detection prompt builder calls it. In src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/tasks/medvision/medvision_utils.py the 16 call sites of `get_resized_img_shape(...)` (lines 1510, 1583, 1657, 1730, 1807, 1893, 1973, 2051, 2121, 2207, 2316, 2432, 2515, 2642, 3354, 3492) all sit inside TumorLesionSize / MaskSize / BiometricsFromLandmarks builders. `create_doc_to_text_BoxCoordinate` (line 683), `create_doc_to_text_BoxCoordinate_CoT` (line 716), `create_doc_to_text_BoxCoordinate_wBox` (line 754) and `doc_to_text_BoxCoordinate_wBox_woMedImg` (line 787) contain no call and state no image/pixel size — the detection prompt is only task text + `FORMAT_PROMPT_BOX_COORDINATES` (medvision_utils.py:739-748). This contradicts the sibling file references/add-a-model.md:24, which correctly says the dispatch branch is "Required for T/L and A/D."
- **Fix:** Drop "detection" from the list: "...called from the T/L, A/D and MaskSize `create_doc_to_text_*` factories (and their CoT / visual-prompt / scaledPS variants). Detection prompts do not call it — detection relies on the perceived canvas only through the client-side pre-resize in the API model files."

*skills:meta (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Claim:** §7: "the snapshot was re-stamped to commit `780e247` on 2026-09-05, and `check_repo_provenance.py` reports `current` against this checkout."
- **Truth:** Running the tool now reports the opposite: `python /mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/.claude/skills/refresh-repo-skill/scripts/check_repo_provenance.py --skill-dir skills/medvision --repo-path .` prints `"status": "stale"` with `"mismatches": ["dirty_paths"]`. skills/medvision/references/repo-provenance.md:24-33 records 8 dirty paths (README.md plus 7 untracked), while `git status --porcelain` now lists 26 modified tracked files (README.md, .readthedocs.yaml, 24 docsite files, script/analyze/clinical-decision-analysis/README.md) plus the same 7 untracked. ### M45. `skills/README.md:268`
*skills:meta (bm) · regression · confirmed*

- **Fix:** Say that one of the two `_CoT` directories IS the roster's MedVision-V0 entry (config-detect-CoT.yaml:4) and only `…__v2_CoT` is off-roster, and describe the failure as test-8's record invariants over every `<model>/llm-parsed_<judge>/` directory it finds, not as a model-folder count.

### M48. `skills/medvision-pipeline/SKILL.md:62`
*skills:meta (bm) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Dataset Download: `python -m medvision_bm.benchmark.download_datasets --data_dir <path> --tasks_json <path_to_tasks_json>` — given with no other prerequisite.
- **Truth:** The command fails as written unless `MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION` is exported first. download_datasets.py:53-57 calls `setup_env_hf_medvision_ds(args.data_dir, …)`, which (install_utils.py:210-236 → setup_env_medvision_ds, install_utils.py:201-209) sets only MedVision_DATA_DIR / MedVision_FORCE_INSTALL_CODE / MedVision_FORCE_DOWNLOAD_DATA — never the planner version; the loader then hard-aborts with "MedVision: annotation version selection required … export MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION=<version>" (/mnt/vincent-pvc-rwm/MedVision/MedVision.py:578-618, raised from _split_generators). The repo's own root skill states this as mandatory (skills/medvision/SKILL.md:53-56: "the loader fails without the latter"), and T/L at annotation 1.0.0 additionally needs MedVision_ACK_RELEASE.
- **Fix:** Add the two exports above the download snippet, e.g. `export MedVision_DATA_DIR=<path>` and `export MedVision_PLANNER_VERSION=1.0.0` (plus `MedVision_ACK_RELEASE=1.4.0` when pinning below the latest release), as skills/medvision/SKILL.md:53-56 does.

### M49. `skills/medvision/sub-skills/results-parsing-and-metrics/references/workflows.md:18`
*skills:parse-judge-analysis (bm) · internal-inconsistency · confirmed*

- **Claim:** "`parse_outputs` imports the vendored eval utilities (`medvision_utils`), pulling in `transformers` and the sibling package `medvision_ds` at module level; the three summarizers import only `torch` and HF `datasets` (via `medvision_bm.utils.__init__`)."
- **Truth:** Two of the three summarizers DO import the vendored eval utilities. src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_TL_task.py:24-26 and src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_AD_task.py:22-24 both do `from medvision_bm.medvision_lmms_eval.lmms_eval.tasks.medvision.medvision_utils import _compute_physical_diagonal`, and medvision_utils.py:7-28 imports `nibabel`, `PIL`, `torch`, `scipy`, `transformers.AutoImageProcessor` and `medvision_ds.utils.doc_to_visual_utils` at module level. Only summarize_detection_task.py (imports at lines 13-40) avoids medvision_utils. The statement also contradicts three sibling statements in the same sub-skill: SKILL.md:15-16 ("both steps import `torch`, `transformers` and `medvision_ds`"), references/troubleshooting.md:10 ("`parse_outputs` and the summarizers import the vendored eval utilities, which import these three at module level"), references/api-reference.md:67-69, and the bundled scripts/parse_and_summarize.sh:150-152, which hard-exits if `import medvision_ds` fails before running EITHER step.
- **Fix:** Replace with something like: "`parse_outputs`, `summarize_TL_task` and `summarize_AD_task` import the vendored eval utilities (`medvision_utils`), pulling in `torch`, `transformers`, `nibabel`, `scipy` and `medvision_ds` at module level; `summarize_detection_task` needs only `torch` and HF `datasets` (via `medvision_bm.utils.__init__`)."

*skills:parse-judge-analysis (bm) · internal-inconsistency · adjusted*

- **Claim:** "The judge needs `vllm==0.19.0` with a Transformers 5.x line, which **most** eval envs cannot host — they pin vLLM 0.10.x-0.14.0. The exception is `requirements_eval_gemma4.txt`, which pins the identical stack; every other eval env is the wrong interpreter for the judge."
- **Truth:** requirements/requirements_eval_glm4v.txt:299 pins `vllm==0.19.1`, which is outside the stated 0.10.x-0.14.0 range, so "every other eval env is the wrong interpreter" and the version range are both inaccurate. The sibling reference file states the correct picture: references/judge-environment.md:138-141 says "Two do not conflict: the gemma4 eval env pins the identical 0.19.0 / transformers 5.10.2 / torch 2.10.0 stack, and glm4v pins vLLM 0.19.1." (Full survey of requirements/: gemma4 0.19.0, glm4v 0.19.1, qwen25vl_update1 0.14.0, qwen3vl 0.11.0, gemma3/llama3_vision 0.10.2, the rest 0.10.0.)
- **Fix:** Align SKILL.md with judge-environment.md §6: say most eval envs pin vLLM 0.10.0-0.14.0, that `requirements_eval_gemma4.txt` pins the identical 0.19.0 stack, and that `requirements_eval_glm4v.txt` pins the nearby 0.19.1 (still not the registry pin `check_judge_env.py` enforces).
- **Verifier adjustment:** skills/medvision/sub-skills/llm-judge-parsing/SKILL.md:95 — category imprecision, severity low. Only the version-range clause is off: eight of the ten eval requirements pin 0.10.0-0.14.0 but requirements_eval_glm4v.txt:299 pins 0.19.1, which the stated range excludes. Fix: '...which most eval envs cannot host — they pin vLLM 0.10.0-0.14.0 (glm4v pins 0.19.1).' Keep 'every other eval env is the wrong interpreter for the judge' as-is: check_judge_env.py exits 2 (MISMATCH) on 0.19.1 against the 0.19.0 registry pin.

## LOW (64)

### L1. `docsite/source/benchmarking/parsing-and-summarizing.md:19`
*docsite:bench (bm) · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Claim:** The on-disk layout is given as `<task_id>_samples_....jsonl` and `<task_id>_results.json` (lines 19-20 and again for the `parsed/` copy at lines 28-29; overview.md line 55 repeats `<task_id>_results.json`), implying the leading filename token is the benchmark task id.
- **Truth:** The leading token is a run timestamp, and the task id is the SUFFIX of the samples file. src/medvision_bm/medvision_lmms_eval/lmms_eval/loggers/evaluation_tracker.py:194 writes `f"{self.date_id}_results.json"` and :242 writes `f"{self.date_id}_samples_{task_name}.jsonl"`, where `date_id` is a datetime string (:193 `self.date_id = datetime_str.replace(":", "-")`). Real files on disk confirm: `Results/MedVision-AD-CoT/LLaVA-Med/20260612_090335_results.json` and `20260612_090335_samples_Ceph-Biometrics-400_BiometricsFromLandmarks_Distance_Task01_Sagittal-CoT.jsonl`. (`parse_outputs._extract_task_id`, src/medvision_bm/benchmark/parse_outputs.py:40-45, shares the misnomer — it regexes everything before `_samples_`, i.e. the timestamp.)
- **Fix:** Write the layout as `<run_timestamp>_samples_<task_id>.jsonl` and `<run_timestamp>_results.json`, so readers grep for the right thing.

### L2. `docsite/source/benchmarking/parsing-and-summarizing.md:34`
*docsite:bench (bm) · wrong-name · confirmed*

- **Claim:** Task-level report is listed as `summary_<task>_task.txt`, where the sibling line 30 uses the same `<task>` placeholder for `summary_metrics_<task>_Task.json` (whose Detection value is `detect`). A reader therefore infers `summary_detect_task.txt`.
- **Truth:** Detection's text report is `summary_detection_task*.txt`, not `summary_detect_task.txt`: src/medvision_bm/benchmark/summarize_detection_task.py:681 `f"summary_detection_task{...}"`. AD/TL do match the placeholder (summarize_AD_task.py:744 `summary_AD_task…`, summarize_TL_task.py:665 `summary_TL_task{_suffix}.txt`), while the JSON constants use `detect` (src/medvision_bm/utils/configs.py:55 `SUMMARY_FILENAME_DETECT_METRICS = "summary_metrics_detect_Task.json"`).
- **Fix:** Spell the three names out, or annotate: `summary_{AD|TL|detection}_task.txt` — note Detection's report says `detection` while its JSONs say `detect`.

### L3. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:64`
*docsite:bench (bm) · stale-behavior · confirmed*

- **Claim:** `--data_dir` | Path to the local `Data/` tree (must match `MedVision_DATA_DIR`).
- **Truth:** The flag does not need to match the env var — it SETS it, unconditionally. src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__qwen2_5_vl.py:296 calls `setup_env_hf_medvision_ds(data_dir)`, which reaches src/medvision_bm/utils/install_utils.py:201 `os.environ["MedVision_DATA_DIR"] = data_dir` (after `os.path.abspath` + `makedirs`). Any pre-existing value is overwritten.

### L4. `docsite/source/benchmarking/running-evaluations.md:337`
*docsite:bench (bm) · coverage-gap · confirmed*

- **Claim:** MiniMax-M3: "`--vllm_version` (default `0.11.0`; the MXFP8/INT4 weights need a vLLM that registers `minimax_m3_vl`)", with an example command that omits `--vllm_version`.
- **Truth:** The argparse default is indeed `0.11.0` (src/medvision_bm/benchmark/eval__minimax_m3.py:188) but the shipped launchers override it because 0.11.0 does not register the architecture: script/benchmark-AD/eval__MiniMax-M3__AD.sh:83 `vllm_version="0.24.0"` and eval__MiniMax-M3-INT4__AD.sh:79 `vllm_version="0.23.0"` (same in the TL and detect launchers), both passed at `--vllm_version "$vllm_version"`. The flag's own help says "The default here is a starting point; if vLLM raises an 'unsupported architecture' error, set this to the release that lands MiniMax-M3-VL support." Copying the doc's example installs 0.11.0.
- **Fix:** Note the launcher pins (`0.24.0` for MXFP8, `0.23.0` for AWQ-INT4) and add `--vllm_version 0.24.0` to the example, or say the `0.11.0` default is a placeholder that must be overridden.
